### Pass 82 — a bare Supabase secret key and access token are redacted

The legacy `service_role` key is a JWT, and the `eyJ` pattern already takes it. The current secret key is not a JWT. Supabase documents `sb_secret_`, 22 base64url characters, `_`, and an 8-character base64url checksum, which is what the generator writes. That key bypasses row-level security. `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY=` was already an assignment, because the name ends in `secret_key`. `supabase status`, the dashboard, and an `apikey` header have no other keyword. No earlier prefix names `sb_secret_`, so the key reached MCP tails and `herdmgr --json`.

A personal access token is account-scoped, and the CLI also accepts it as a database password. The CLI's pattern is `sbp_`, optionally `v0_` or `oauth_`, plus 40 lowercase hex characters. `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN=` was already an assignment. `supabase login` and a pasted token were not. `v` and `o` are not hex, so the plain `sbp_` pattern does not match those two forms.

The prefix stays. A second pass counts 0. `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY=`, `apikey`, a JSON value, a URL password, and `Authorization: Bearer` keep the label and count once, including a lowercase scheme. The publishable key stays. A body that is not exactly that shape stays whole, so a tail is not left behind. The same holds for a token that is not exactly 40 lowercase hex characters. `sbp_v0_` and `sbp_oauth_` keep their version on the label.

Why this one: backlog items 1–84 stay deferred for the reasons already recorded. Item 84 is the length floor on the age identity pass 81 added. This is the credential those tails were built to hide, missed once Supabase stopped issuing the service-role JWT. One secret key reads and writes the project. One access token reaches every project on the account.

The organization pattern is listed first, so a payload is not a second secret.

This is the credential those tails were built to hide, missed once Sentry stopped issuing a bare hex token. One personal token reads the user's projects. One organization token ships source maps.

- A transcription of the pattern agreed with those boundaries: 22 `A`s and 43 `b`s match, and so does a body whose segments contain `-` and `_`. 21 and 23 in the first segment do not. 42 and 44 in the second do not, and those strings stay whole. A `+`, `/`, or `=` in either segment does not. `sg.` does not. A glued letter or underscore does not. `SG.` plus a hyphenated note does not. A hyphen before the prefix leaves the key. A period after it leaves that mark. Two keys count as two. The published sample does not contain `eyJ`, and a real key's only dots are the two separators, so the JWT pattern, which needs two dots after its header, does not see it. No earlier pattern matched these strings.
- `SENDGRID_API_KEY=` and `{"token":"…"}` keep the prefix and count once. A value of 8 characters under that name is still an assignment, and 7 characters stay. Bearer keeps that label and counts once. A URL password keeps the user and the host. `token=<key>@leftover` counts twice and becomes `token=[REDACTED]`: `@` is still not the end of an ordinary value. A second pass on each of those counts 0.
- Not compiled and not run as a Swift package. No Swift toolchain on this box.

### Pass 86 — a bare DigitalOcean API token is redacted

DigitalOcean issues three shapes, and the body of each is 64 lowercase hex characters: `dop_v1_` for a personal token from the control panel, `doo_v1_` for an OAuth access token, and `dor_v1_` for the refresh token that mints another. That personal token can manage the account. `DIGITALOCEAN_TOKEN=` was already an assignment, because the name ends in `token`. `doctl`, a curl line, and a pasted token have no keyword. No earlier prefix names these, so the token reached MCP tails and `herdmgr --json`.

The prefix stays. A second pass counts 0. `DIGITALOCEAN_TOKEN=`, a JSON `access_token` and `refresh_token`, a URL password, an OAuth callback fragment, and `Authorization: Bearer` keep the label and count once, including a lowercase scheme. A body that is not exactly 64 lowercase hex stays whole, and so does an uppercase prefix. A legacy 64-hex token with no prefix stays.

Why this one: backlog items 1–88 stay deferred for the reasons already recorded. Item 88 is the length floor on the SendGrid key pass 85 added. This is the credential those tails were built to hide, missed for the token a `doctl` pane prints beside the account. One personal token manages the droplets. One refresh token mints another.

### Pass 130 — a silence reading does not sample the shell just because it is listed last

herdr's `foreground_processes` follows `proc_listpids` on macOS and the procfs group walk on Linux. That order is not "the agent is last". The silence check ran `ps` on `.last`. When the pane shell was last, its CPU is idle while `node` or `bun` is the agent, so the menu bar said a busy agent was stalled and MCP suggested `agent.interrupt` for a deadlock that was the shell.

The sample now skips a bare shell. A runtime whose script is an agent outranks the shell that launched it. A process whose own name is the agent outranks a runtime that is not that script, such as an MCP `node server.js` in the same group. Any other non-shell outranks the shell. The same rank prefers a runtime over a shell interpreter, then the lower pid, so a helper spawned later does not hide the process that started the group. A group that is only a bare shell is unknown CPU, the same answer as an empty read. The shell that is still interpreting an agent is the sample when nothing else is in the group.

Why this one: backlog items 1–92 stay deferred for the reasons already recorded. Item 92 is the relative-path floor from pass 129. This is the pid the stalled-agent warning is based on, and the list order herdr sends is not the order the sample assumed.

A recognized `xai-` or `ghp_` value keeps that label and counts once. A URL with no userinfo stays, including a port and `git@host:path`. A password of 7 characters stays (item 49). An assignment whose value is the whole URL is still one redaction (pass 46). `Authorization: Basic` and `Proxy-Authorization: Basic` are redacted, including a JSON `"Authorization": "Basic …"` value and the single-quoted form. The header's case stays. There may be no space between the scheme and the token. The token is standard base64 of at least 8 characters, padded or not, and it has to carry a digit, `+`, `/`, or `=`, or two capitals and two lowercase letters (`user:pass` is `dXNlcjpwYXNz`, which has no digit). A second pass counts 0. A recognized `xai-` or `ghp_` value in that slot keeps its label and counts once. `Authorization: Basic authentication` and a single capital (`Password`) stay (pass 47). `agent.tail` accepts `visible`, `recent`, `recent_unwrapped`, and `detection` after trim and case-folding. A missing argument, including a non-string value, is still detection. Blank and any other word are an error before the socket call, instead of the detection buffer. A hyphen or a missing underscore (`recent-unwrapped`, `RecentUnwrapped`) stays an error, because those are not the wire name (pass 48). A `session.spawn` brief is sent only when the new pane is idle, working, or done on the list taken after the startup wait. Blocked does not receive it: the prompt submits Enter, and that used to accept a startup permission prompt. The spawn still returns the new pane, and `briefSent` is false with a fixed reason. A brief longer than 2000 characters is refused before any pane is created (pass 49). `agent.say` checks `wait_for` before any text is sent. `idle`, `done`, and `blocked` are accepted after trim and case-folding. Blank, `working`, `unknown`, and any other word are an error, and so is a timeout below 1ms or above 25000ms. A missing argument still does not wait, and a timeout beside it is ignored. The old default of 30000 is refused, because it meets the request socket and used to fail after the prompt was already submitted. A wait that throws after the send returns `sent: true` and `outcome: wait_failed` (pass 50). herdr's status timeout is not that throw: it is `outcome: timeout` (pass 58). `session.spawn`'s `agent.start` timeout is still 30000 (item 57). A timeout after the pane exists returns that pane with `started` false instead of failing the tool (pass 55). The wake wait before a brief uses the 25000 cap. herdr's status timeout returns unsettled rather than throwing, so that expiry reaches the list (pass 58). A connect failure still does not. A throw from that wait, from the herd list after it, or from the brief's prompt no longer fails the spawn: the pane is returned, Enter is not sent unless the list says idle, working, or done, and a prompt that does not finish cleanly is not reported as sent (pass 51). Enter's failure after the text write is no longer `invalidResponse`. `agent.say` returns `textInserted` and `enter_unconfirmed` instead of a failed tool, and it does not start the wait. A nudge says the text was inserted. A spawn brief is still not reported as sent (pass 52). A confirmed `agent.say`, `agent.interrupt`, or `agent.stop` that followed a move now names that pane as `resolvedAgentId`. The auto-send path and the Enter-unconfirmed result already did. A quote, a backslash, or a control character in the id is escaped, so the hand-built object stays one JSON value (pass 53). `session.spawn`'s result escapes the pane id, the workspace id, the tab id, the placement, and the action id the same way. A quote in one of them used to end the object, so the caller had no id for an agent that had already started (pass 54). Once `session.spawn` has created a pane, a shell that never appears, a closed write gate, or an `agent.start` that throws no longer fails the tool. The result names that pane, `started` is false, and a requested brief is not sent (pass 55). A herdr rejection of the brief's text write now says the brief was not sent. An oversized success line stays unconfirmed, because the text may already be in the pane (pass 56). The bearer scheme is matched in either case, and a compact JWT or JWE with no scheme is redacted. A second pass does not count it again. A segment shorter than 10 characters, an empty segment, and a scheme glued to the previous word stay (pass 57). A herdr `agent.wait` timeout is an unsettled wait. `agent.say` reports `timeout`, and a spawn brief still reads the herd list (pass 58). A message that is not the exact timeout sentence still throws (item 64). `herdmgr --json` now prints `needs_you` as a boolean, the full kind, the directory, and a redacted name, workspace, tab, and kind (pass 59). `state_change_seq` and `priority` stay strings. The live table still fits the kind into 12 columns, and a longer kind shows an ellipsis. `--json` still lists every agent; `--show-all` only widens the live table (item 65). A URL username that ends in an assignment keyword keeps the host when the password is one the URL pattern redacts (pass 60). A prefix longer than 39 characters, a keyword inside the password, a short password, and a raw `@` still take the host (item 66). A bare GitLab token (`glpat-`, `gldt-`, `glrt-`, `glrtr-`, `glcbt-`, `glptt-`, `gloas-`, `glagent-`, `glsoat-`, `glffct-`, `glimt-`, `glft-`, `gltok-`) is redacted and the prefix stays. A second pass counts 0. A body shorter than 20, a `.` or `=` inside the body, an instance prefix that does not contain one of those, and a `_gitlab_session` cookie stay (item 67). A Slack incoming, workflow, or trigger webhook URL is redacted and the host stays, including GovSlack, a bare host, an `http` copy, and `SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL`. A second pass counts 0. `token=<url>` keeps the host. A last segment shorter than 16, a segment shorter than 8, a docs path with no token, and a host that only looks like `hooks.slack.com` stay (item 68). `xapp-`, `xoxe.xoxb-`, `xoxe.xoxp-`, `xoxe-`, and `xoxc-` are redacted and the prefix stays, including a base64 `+`, `/`, or `=` that used to leave a tail or skip the token. A second pass counts 0. Discord and Teams webhook URLs, including a Logic App `sig`, keep a host (pass 63). `xoxd-`, `xwfp-`, `xoxo-`, a short body, and a path that is not the documented shape stay (item 70). Stripe restricted keys (`rk_live_`, `rk_test_`) and `whsec_` webhook secrets are redacted and the prefix stays (pass 64). A body shorter than 20, a publishable `pk_` key, a `+` after a restricted key, and a `whsec_` body that continues with `-` or `_` stay (item 71). `secretKey`, `SecretAccessKey`, `privateKey`, and `passwordKey` are assignments, including when no separator is present (pass 65). `secretKeys`, `secretAccessKeyId`, `privateKeys`, and `passwordKeyboard` stay. An `ASIA` access key id in that same dump stays (item 73). A Google API key (`AIza` plus 35) and an OAuth client secret (`GOCSPX-` plus 28) are redacted and the prefix stays (pass 66). A body that is not that length, a `ya29.` access token, and a `googleusercontent.com` client id stay (item 74). A workspace or tab focus is not a layout refetch, and it is not subscribed (pass 67). herdmgr does not arm the move-dwell baseline from a click, and it does not read every process or clear the screen for a focus, a new shell, or an event that leaves the rows alone. `pane.updated` still reads processes when the visible row did not change. `pane.agent_detected` still does not insert a row (item 75). A bare npm publish token (`npm_` plus 36) and a PyPI upload token (`pypi-AgEIcHlwaS5vcmc` plus at least 50) are redacted and the prefix stays (pass 68). A Hugging Face user token (`hf_` plus 34 letters), an organization token (`api_org_` plus 34 letters), and a RubyGems API key (`rubygems_` plus 48 lowercase hex) are redacted and the prefix stays (pass 69). An npm body that is not 36 characters, an uppercase `NPM_`, a PyPI body shorter than 50 or longer than 1000, a `.` that ends a PyPI body, a Hugging Face body that is not 34 letters, and a RubyGems body that is not 48 lowercase hex stay (item 77). An OpenAI admin key (`sk-admin-` plus at least 20) is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 70). `Authorization: Bearer` in front of a token that already has a label keeps that label and counts once, including `xai-`, `ghp_`, `sk-proj-`, and `sk-admin-`. An opaque bearer token and a JWT after the scheme are still one `Bearer [REDACTED]`. A body shorter than 20, an uppercase `SK-ADMIN-`, and a letter or underscore glued to the front stay (item 79). A crashed pane whose shell is nushell (`nu`), PowerShell (`pwsh`, `powershell`, including `.exe`), or `csh` is now gone, including a login argv0 and a path (pass 71). A shell whose `argv` launches a known agent stays running, and so does `cmdline` when `argv` was not sent: `sh /path/to/pi`, `powershell -File claude.ps1`, and `cmd /C codex.cmd` (pass 72). `dash`, `ksh`, `csh`, and `tcsh` do too, and so does a program that follows `-o`, `-O`, or `--rcfile` on the shells that take that flag (pass 73). A cluster does too: `bash -euo pipefail` and `bash -oeu pipefail` leave the script as the program, and so do `+o` and `+O` on the shells that have them. fish's `-d`, `--profile`, and `--init-command` take a value, and `-d3` keeps that value in the word. `ash` and `mksh` are crash shells and unwrap a script the way dash does. `bash -oerrexit` takes the next word as the option name (pass 74). A PowerShell parameter value is not the program (pass 75): `-InputFormat Text`, `-ep Bypass`, `-o XML`, `-wd`, and `-File:claude.ps1` still launch the script, `/File` is `-File`, and `-e` stays an encoded blob. `sh -c`, `nu` with a later path, an encoded PowerShell command, and a `cmd.exe` with no argument vector stay (item 80). `tmux` and a name that only starts with `nu` stay. A basename of `cli.js` stays a crash unless the path is an npm entrypoint herdr names (pass 77): Pi's `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent` `dist/cli.js` and `dist/bundle/cli.js`, omp's `@oh-my-pi` `dist/cli.js`, Kimi's `dist/main.mjs`, Qwen's `dist/index.js`, and Mastracode's `dist/cli.js`. `setup.js`, `cli.exe`, a short `dist/bundle/cli.js`, and `cli.js` with another file after it stay. `sh -c` still does not read the path, and `nu` still does not unwrap it. `elvish` and `xonsh` are the pane shell herdr names (pass 76), including a login argv0, a path, and `.exe`. A script path on either stays a crash, the same as `nu`. A name that only begins with either word stays. A bare Groq key (`gsk_` plus 52 alphanumeric characters) is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 78). A shorter body, a longer body, an uppercase `GSK_`, a letter or underscore glued to the front, and a hyphen inside the body stay (item 81). `GROQ_API_KEY` is already an assignment, so a shorter value under that name is still redacted. `Authorization: Bearer` keeps the `gsk_` label and counts once. A bare Perplexity key (`pplx-` plus 48 alphanumeric characters) is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 79). A shorter body, a longer body, an uppercase `PPLX-`, a letter or underscore glued to the front, a hyphen inside the body, and a model name such as `pplx-70b` or `pplx-api` stay (item 82). `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY` is already an assignment, so a shorter value under that name is still redacted. `Authorization: Bearer` keeps the `pplx-` label and counts once. A bare Doppler token (`dp.ct.`, `dp.pt.`, `dp.sa.`, `dp.said.`, `dp.scim.`, `dp.audit.`, and `dp.st.` with an optional lowercase config of 2 to 35 characters) plus 40 to 44 alphanumeric characters is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 80). A shorter body, a longer body, an uppercase prefix, a letter or underscore glued to the front, a hyphen inside the body, a one-character config, an uppercase config, and a config longer than 35 stay (item 83). `DOPPLER_TOKEN` is already an assignment, so a shorter value under that name is still redacted. `Authorization: Bearer` keeps the `dp.st.` or `dp.ct.` label and counts once. A bare age identity (`AGE-SECRET-KEY-1` plus 58 Bech32 characters) is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 81). A shorter body, a longer body, a `B`, `I`, `O`, `1`, or hyphen inside the body, a lowercase prefix, and a letter or underscore glued to the front stay (item 84). The public key (`age1…`) stays. `AGE_SECRET_KEY` is already an assignment, so a shorter value under that name is still redacted. `Authorization: Bearer` keeps the `AGE-SECRET-KEY-1` label and counts once. A bare Supabase secret key (`sb_secret_` plus 22 base64url characters, `_`, and 8 base64url characters) is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 82). A personal access token (`sbp_`, `sbp_v0_`, or `sbp_oauth_` plus 40 lowercase hex characters) is redacted and that prefix stays. A body that is not that shape stays whole, including a longer checksum and a `+`, `/`, or `=` inside the secret key (item 85). The publishable key stays. `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` and `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` are already assignments, so a shorter value under those names is still redacted. `Authorization: Bearer` keeps the `sb_secret_` or `sbp_` label and counts once. A bare Sentry personal token (`sntryu_` plus 64 lowercase hex characters) is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 83). An organization token (`sntrys_`, base64 of a JSON object that starts with `{"iat":` and names `region_url`, then `_` and a 43-character secret) is redacted and `sntrys_` stays. A user body that is not exactly 64 lowercase hex characters stays whole, and so does an organization token whose payload segment is outside 10 to 200 characters or whose secret is not exactly 43 characters (item 86). `sntrya_` and `sntryi_` stay. `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` is already an assignment, so a shorter value under that name is still redacted. `Authorization: Bearer` keeps the `sntryu_` or `sntrys_` label and counts once. A bare LangSmith personal token (`lsv2_pt_` plus at least 32 alphanumeric characters, then zero or more underscore-delimited segments) is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 84). A service key (`lsv2_sk_` and the same body) is redacted and that prefix stays. A first segment shorter than 32 stays whole, including when a later segment is long, and so does a hyphen inside that first segment (item 87). `ls__` stays. `LANGSMITH_API_KEY` is already an assignment, so a shorter value under that name is still redacted. `Authorization: Bearer` keeps the `lsv2_pt_` or `lsv2_sk_` label and counts once. A bare SendGrid API key (`SG.` plus 22 characters, `.`, and 43 characters) is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 85). A segment that is not those lengths stays whole, and so does a `+`, `/`, or `=` inside either segment (item 88). A lowercase `sg.` stays. `SENDGRID_API_KEY` is already an assignment, so a shorter value under that name is still redacted. `Authorization: Bearer` keeps the `SG.` label and counts once. A bare DigitalOcean token (`dop_v1_`, `doo_v1_`, or `dor_v1_` plus 64 lowercase hex characters) is redacted and the prefix stays (pass 86). A body that is not exactly 64 lowercase hex characters stays whole, and so does an uppercase prefix and a legacy 64-hex token with no prefix (item 89). `DIGITALOCEAN_TOKEN` is already an assignment, so a shorter value under that name is still redacted. `Authorization: Bearer` keeps the `dop_v1_`, `doo_v1_`, or `dor_v1_` label and counts once. A shell whose program basename is `Kimi Code`, `Qwen Code`, `Letta Code`, `Kilo Code`, `Mastra Code`, or `Devin CLI` is that agent, including `.exe`, `.cmd`, `.bat`, `.ps1`, and `.js` (pass 87). A longer basename stays a crash (item 90). A Shopify access token (`shpat_`), custom-app token (`shpca_`), private or delegate token (`shppa_`), app secret (`shpss_`), and refresh token (`shprt_`) are redacted and the prefix stays (pass 88). A body that is not exactly 32 lowercase hex characters stays whole, and so does an uppercase prefix (item 91). An absolute program path that is a symlink to an agent basename is that agent, including `agent` → `cursor-agent`, `muse-bin-<version>`, a spaced name, and one `.js` suffix (pass 128). A relative path that contains a slash is joined to that process's `cwd` from `pane.process_info` and canonicalized the same way, including `./agent`, `bin/launcher`, `sub/../agent`, and a trailing slash on the cwd (pass 129). A bare name, a missing path, a path with no cwd, a Windows path, and a package path that appears only on the target stay a crash (item 92). A silence reading no longer samples whichever foreground process was listed last: a bare shell is not the pid, a runtime whose script is an agent outranks the shell that launched it, and a process whose own name is the agent outranks a runtime that is not that script (pass 130). The process herdr names from argv[0] (a nix wrapper, `MainThread` with `opencode.exe`) and Windows Cursor's `node.exe` + `index.js` bundle now outrank that shell and a helper runtime (pass 131). Two plain runtimes still share a rank, and the lower pid is the sample (item 93). A plain `node` beside `index.js` is not that bundle (item 94).

1. A seq-less `pane_updated` that arrives after a poll already applied a newer status still flips the pane. The event carries no seq, so it cannot be told apart from a genuine second prompt. Deferred again: any rule that drops a seq-less status change also drops the live update the subscription exists to deliver. A non-zero seq is already ignored when it is behind the stored one.
2. Done in pass 34. The dwell file stores the session beside the kind fingerprint. A relaunch restores the clock only when a session named on both sides is the same string. A different session does not inherit the dwell. A file written before the field existed has none, and still matches on kind, status, and a non-zero seq. A live row whose list left the field off does too, and the restore writes that session onto the row, so the next different one opens an episode. The settings key is still `DwellTracker.fingerprint` / `AppModel.fingerprintForAgent`, kind only. What remains: a pane that never had a session, and a restart that comes back with the same session at the same seq. herdr has no instance id for the second, and an empty value is still not an occupant.
3. `paneBasis` keeps a tombstone for a pane a status, presence, or move event touched, including the previous id after a cross-workspace move, so a late pre-event list cannot resurrect it. Pane ids are not reused; the map grows with ids seen this launch. Deferred: one or two structs per moved pane per launch is not worth a pruning rule that might drop a basis an in-flight read still needs.
4. A herdr restart that reuses a pane id and comes back at the same `state_change_seq` with the same occupant fingerprint does not reset the 3-answer cap. Deferred: that read looks exactly like a second observation of the episode the cap is counting. Resetting on an equal seq would let a stale read clear the cap, which the policy test forbids. A lower seq, or the same seq with a new occupant fingerprint on the same budget key, does reset. A different session identity is its own budget (pass 18). A title that is `""` is the same fallback fingerprint as a missing title when the name or terminal title agrees (pass 31). An `agent_session` whose value is `""` is that same fallback, not a session fingerprint (pass 35).
5. Done in pass 7. A connect failure, a refresh that fails, and a dropped subscription clear the protocol under the enqueue epoch. A herd read that already started cannot record over that clear. A read that starts afterwards still can. `setLatestProtocol` with a nil epoch still bypasses that floor; no request path calls it that way.
6. Done in pass 22. A gated `agent.say` lists the herd again after the policy check and addresses that list. The original pane wins when it is still the same occupant. A different occupant sends nothing and does not take a slot. A pane that is no longer an agent follows a unique session, and only while that pane is still idle or done. `prompt` submits Enter, so a block or a working agent in the gap does not receive it. Idle and done still receive the text when the seq changes. An empty session value does not follow, and `abc` versus `abc|extra` does not either. A move during `reserveWrite`, after that list, still addresses the id the list named. That write is already queued.
7. `HerdrAgentInfo.==` does not compare `agentSession` (`AgentSession` is not `Equatable`) or `paneLabel`. `AnswerSendCheck` uses the fingerprint, not `==`. The store, the snapshot merge, and the write path do not branch on `HerdrAgentInfo` equality, so the missing fields do not change behavior today. Deferred: making `AgentSession` equatable is safe, and still has no caller. The row's name is what a pane-label change updates.
8. Done in pass 8. herdmgr's layout refetch no longer leaves a moved pane on a fresh dwell. The move copies the pre-refetch `enteredAt` onto the new id when status and seq still match, including when several panes move in one burst. A refetch after one of those moves has been applied starts a new baseline; a later move whose previous id is only on the old baseline keeps the reset dwell. A status or seq change is not restored. Shepherd still does not refetch on those events. A workspace or tab focus is not one of those refetches (pass 67), so a click does not arm the baseline.
9. A seq-less `pane_moved` does not adopt a status that appears only on that payload. Deferred: `PaneInfo` has no seq, so a live status and a replayed one are the same bytes. Applying the payload status is what made a protocol-17 buffer replay reopen a blocked episode. `pane.updated` and Shepherd's poll still carry status. herdmgr still ignores the status on that payload. Once the row is on the id the table shows, the 3s poll applies the list's status (pass 29). A status that exists only inside the move waits for that poll, or for `pane_updated`.
10. A seq-less `pane_moved` with no agent still drops a row whose id matches. A replayed same-id move-to-shell can remove an agent that started in that pane later. Deferred: a live move to a shell has the same shape, and leaving the row would stick in herdmgr. Shepherd's next poll inserts the agent again.
11. A mutation already queued ahead of the snapshot that records a downgrade still runs. Deferred: the I/O queue is serial, and that write started when the gate was still open. Waiting for a read that has not been enqueued would stall every keystroke on a snapshot nobody requested. Pass 9 refuses the write once the downgrade's transaction is ahead of it.
12. Done in pass 11. The last usage event stays out of the history fold, with the unterminated tail. A Claude message that is still the last usage line keeps its id when a week or month boundary folds the older lines, and the next copy replaces it. The older lines in that session still fold.
13. A repeated Claude usage line that is no longer the last usage event can still be folded and then counted twice. An edit of bytes behind the resume signature is still unseen. Deferred: both sit behind a newer line, or behind bytes the append reader does not visit. Keeping every id that has ever been last would stop the older history from folding.
14. Done in pass 12. A silence measured at the start of a diagnosis pass is measured again on the row as it is when the verdict lands. Output inside the threshold leaves the pane healthy, including a quiet it was already showing. The heartbeat's newer output time clears that quiet immediately. A time that is still before the episode does not. Process-gone is unchanged.
15. A diagnosis result for a pane that moved while the pass was in flight is dropped. The old id is gone, and the new id keeps the verdict from before the pass until the next one. Deferred again: pass 15 records the id change, but the read was issued against the old pane id. After the move that id can be a bare shell, and stamping process-gone onto the new id would mark a live agent gone. The next pass reads the new id.
16. Done in pass 13. A crashed pane is its own bucket, mark (`GONE`), and footer count. herdmgr reads the process list before paint, after events, and every 15s, so a dead process is not shown as working or blocked. A failed read does not clear a crash. MCP uses the same mark and does not count a crash as unknown as well. The panel's Running tab and header use that same distinction (pass 40): a quiet worker stays running, and a crash on a finished pane is not counted as done.
17. herdmgr does not classify silence. Deferred: the table has no output clock. Timing silence from `enteredAt` would mark a busy agent quiet at the 5-minute threshold. The menu bar's heartbeat is what makes that clock real. MCP's diagnosing tools keep an episode clock and a detection baseline across calls in that process (pass 21). herdmgr's live table still does not.
18. A poll that lands before `pane.moved` still starts a new episode when the pane has no `agent_session.value`. Pass 15 carries the episode only when exactly one dropped pane and one new pane share a non-empty session value. Deferred: kind and title are shared by agents that are not the same occupant, and an empty value is what several panes look like. Selection and the detection hash still follow when the event arrives, because the new id is already a row. The duplicate blocked alert and the reset dwell for that session-less pane do not.
19. A detection read that finishes after the hash was already moved still records on the old id and does not carry a change. The next poll of the new id reports a screen that differs from the hash the move saved. Deferred: the read was addressed to the old pane id, and after the move those bytes can be the shell that replaced the agent. Parking them would clear a silence for output the agent did not produce. Pass 16 carries only a change the poll had already compared against the mover's own previous screen. A later replace of that same hop no longer installs the first look on the destination (pass 20). The read itself is unchanged. MCP's diagnosis poll is tagged with the herd serial (pass 24): once a newer read has noted its serial, the older poll drops those bytes instead of storing them. A Shepherd read is not tagged, and it is still the pane id the poll started with.
20. `agent.answer` still refuses when its pane id is gone, including when the same session is blocked on the same seq at a new id. `explain` was addressed to the old pane. After the move that id can be a shell, and the block kind that justified the keys is not a reading of the new pane. Deferred: the explain gap is short. The consecutive-answer cap follows the session (pass 18), so addressing the new id spends the same budget. Confirm-tier writes follow the session; they do not explain a screen. The refusal stays locked by `AnswerSendCheck`'s gone-pane test.
21. Close, Nudge, and Jump still address the pane id from the click. Deferred: they do not hold that id across a confirmation wait. A move during the one socket call is the write that was already queued. Close needs its own rule for whether a status change still means that occupant. Gated `agent.say` re-reads and follows a unique idle or done session (pass 22). A move after that list is the queued write in item 6. The reserve records the cooldown on the pane that was addressed and on its session.
22. The moved row's Approve button stays enabled while the original click is in flight. The click is ignored, and the footer says a response is already being sent. Deferred: disabling the button means the in-flight id set has to follow every later hop and be removed only by the task that started it. The session set already stops the second Enter.
23. A pane with no `agent_session.value` still gets a fresh answer cap and write cooldown when its id changes. Deferred: an empty value is what several panes look like, and kind plus title are not an occupant. Pass 18 follows only a non-empty session identity. The pane that was actually written stays in cooldown for 10s either way.
24. Answers recorded before a session value existed stay on the pane id. The first `recordStatusChange` that carries a session starts that session's budget at zero and clears the pane mirror on the id it observed. Deferred: treating that as the same occupant would also adopt a previous occupant's count when a new session reuses the pane at the same seq, which is the reset pass 4 exists to do. A session that is present for the whole episode is the path pass 18 covers.
25. Done in pass 20. A poll that continues several sessions replaces every detection hash in one call. A second replace of a hop that already moved does not clear the destination, and does not install a hash the old id recorded after the move. A never-polled origin still clears. Prune keeps that record while the new id is in the herd. A single move was already safe; this is the later pane in the same snapshot.
26. `fingerprintBelongs` treats a session value that continues with `|` (`abc` versus `abc|extra`) as the same occupant. `abc` versus `abcd` does not, and that case is tested. Deferred: the budget key is the identity string itself, and MCP passes the fingerprint and the identity from the same `HerdrAgentInfo`, so the two values are different keys. The prefix check only runs for fingerprints stored on that same key. The diagnosis episode clock compares the identity strings exactly, so this prefix does not join two clocks (pass 21).
27. MCP blocked and quiet durations start when that process first sees the episode. herdr has no wall clock to backdate them. A call that never got a detection read does not start the silence clock. Quiet begins only after a later read still sees that same screen, past the threshold. A gap long enough for the detection buffer to scroll can match an older hash and report quiet. Deferred: writing the clock into Shepherd's dwell file would share that file with no cross-process lock. The first call in a process reporting 0s is the observation, not a claim about the time before the server started.

Pass 23 refuses that pane when a session was captured and the re-read names a different one, or names none. Deferred: an empty session value is what several panes look like, and kind is not an occupant. A restart that comes back with the same session value and the same seq is still the observation item 4 describes. There is no second fact to refuse on.
29. herdmgr's status poll does not add or remove rows. A pane `agent.list` dropped stays until `pane.updated`, `pane.closed`, `pane.exited`, or a layout refetch. Deferred: a move's new id is already in that list before `pane.moved`, and adopting the list would drop the pre-move row. The layout refetch would then remember the reset dwell. `pane.updated` still inserts an agent whose name just appeared, and drops one whose agent field is empty. The 15s process scan still marks a bare shell `GONE`.
30. A cleared rename sticks when that list also omits the metadata title, `display_agent`, and the terminal title. Deferred: a list with no display field is the same shape as one that left the title off, and pass 29 keeps the row's name in that case so a partial list cannot blank it. A list that still carries the terminal title, or a `display_agent`, shows that instead of the cleared rename. `display_agent` is not remembered on its own: the next event that leaves it off falls back to the rename, which is the field pane events do not carry. A real pane event includes `display_agent` when herdr has one.
31. Done in pass 32. herdmgr's status poll returns the rows whose status, seq, or session opened an episode, and reads the process list for those rows before it draws. A bare shell stamps `GONE` on the new episode, so a dead agent whose status string stayed `working` stays on the attention list. A same-episode poll returns nothing and is not read, so it cannot clear a crash the last scan stamped. A pane the list does not contain is not returned. An empty process read leaves the status verdict; the 15s tick still reads the whole table, which is also how a crash with no episode change is found. Done and idle are named when their episode opens, and that read returns running before any socket call. Shepherd still requests a diagnosis pass on the transition.

Putting either in the string would treat a metadata change as a new occupant, and fingerprints already stored on pending actions do not include them. The fingerprint's fallback is a nonempty title, then the rename, then the terminal title, then the kind (pass 31). The row's name uses the pane label between the rename and the terminal title (pass 39).

A herdr error is the same, because the response was read and the start was rejected. A timeout and an oversized success line stay unconfirmed: the agent may be running.
